<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct exact nonuniform Fourier circuits of size $`o(n\log n)`$ along an unbounded sequence of lengths, counting every addition, subtraction, and scalar multiplication. This refutes the $`\Omega(n\log n)`$ lower bound in the unrestricted complex linear-circuit model. The construction uses a finite tensor saving: a tensor power of some invertible nonmonomial complex matrix can be computed with fewer matrix calls than the standard tensor-axis algorithm on the same coordinates, when invertible monomial maps are allowed freely between calls.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): In this model, $$\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{L(n)}{n\log_2 n}=0.$$ Equivalently, for every $c>0$ and every integer $N_0\ge2$, there is an integer $n\ge N_0$ and an exact circuit for $F_n$ with fewer than $c n\log_2 n$ gates.
  - Explanation: The normalized scalar gate count has vanishing limit infimum along an unbounded sequence in the unrestricted exact nonuniform model.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-generation)): Let $A\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_q(\mathbb C)$ be nonmonomial. There is an integer $h>0$ such that every $H\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_q(\mathbb C)$ admits a factorization $$\label{gen:eq:common-pattern} H=M_0 A M_1 A\cdots A M_h,$$ with exactly $h$ occurrences of $A$ and monomial matrices $M_i$. In particular, identity admits such a factorization with a positive number of calls. More generally, for every fixed $w\geq q$, there is a fixed positive pattern on $w$ coordinates realizing every element of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_w(\mathbb C)$ by embedded calls to $A$ and monomials.
  - Explanation: A fixed positive call pattern realizes every invertible target of the fixed width by adjusting its monomial factors.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-amplification)): If $(A,b,g)$ and a specified winning word form a finite win, then there are constants $C<\infty$ and $0<\alpha<1$ such that $A^{\otimes k}$ has an exact scalar linear circuit with at most $Cq^k(k+1)^\alpha$ gates for every integer $k\geq0$.
  - Explanation: A specified strict finite saving amplifies to a sublinear dependence on tensor order after charging scalar gates.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-fft-dirty-layers)): Let $M:\mathbb C^e\to\mathbb C^a$ have a linear DAG with $\ell$ gates and depth $H$. Suppose every variable input and gate has fan-out at most $\delta$, counting designated output uses. On disjoint source coordinates $x$, target coordinates $y$, and $\ell$ further coordinates $z$, there is a word of coordinate shears realizing $$(x,y,z)\longmapsto(x,y+Mx,z).$$ It uses at most $8\ell+2a$ shears and $O\bigl((\delta+1)(H+1)\bigr)$ pair layers. The initial values of $z$ are arbitrary, and may depend on $x,y$ or other data.
  - Explanation: Forward replay, reverse replay and cancellation add the linear output while restoring arbitrary borrowed values.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-fft-toeplitz-layers)): Every invertible lower triangular Toeplitz matrix of width $r$ has a factorization on exactly $r$ coordinates into $O(1+\log^4 r)$ monomial and pair layers. The implied constant is absolute and independent of its entries.
  - Explanation: Low displacement rank and restored internal workspace yield shallow Toeplitz factorizations on the original coordinates.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-layers)): For every integer $r\ge1$, the unnormalized Fourier matrix $F_r$ has a factorization on exactly $r$ coordinates into $O(1+\log^4r)$ monomial and pair layers, with an absolute implied constant.
  - Explanation: Newton interpolation and triangular Toeplitz factors give an exact Fourier factorization without widening the coordinate set.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-win-to-fourier)): If a finite win exists, there are integers $n_m\to\infty$ and exact linear circuits for $F_{n_m}$ whose scalar gate counts satisfy $$L(n_m)=o(n_m\log_2n_m).$$ In particular the conclusion is in the model charging additions, subtractions, and multiplication by every prechosen complex scalar.
  - Explanation: Synchronization across distinct prime factors transfers any supplied finite win to a subsequence of exact Fourier circuits.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-prices)): There is a function $$p:\bigcup_{n\geq1}\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(\mathbb C)\longrightarrow[0,\infty)$$ which vanishes exactly on the monomial matrices and has the following properties. For invertible matrices of the indicated sizes, $$\begin{aligned} p(LAR)&=p(A) &&\text{if $L,R$ are monomial of width $\|A\|$}, \label{price:monomial-law}\\ p(AB)&\leq p(A)+p(B) &&\text{if $\|A\|=\|B\|$}, \label{price:product-law}\\ p(A\otimes B)&=\|B\|p(A)+\|A\|p(B), \label{price:tensor-law}\\ p(A\oplus B)&=p(A)+p(B), \label{price:sum-law}\\ p(A^{-1})&=p(A^{\mathsf T})=p(A). \label{price:symmetry-law} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$p(U)=1,\qquad U=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\1&1\end{pmatrix}. \label{price:normalization}$$
  - Explanation: Assuming no finite win, separation and compactness construct an auxiliary price satisfying the displayed algebraic laws.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-price-packing)): Let $\Delta^{(1)},\ldots,\Delta^{(R)}\in\mathbb R^m$ be any finite family of comparison vectors, with $m\geq1$ and the type list containing all their nonmonomial factors and calls. Under the no-win assumption there are no numbers $\lambda_r\geq0$ such that $$s_i:=\sum_{r=1}^R\lambda_r\Delta^{(r)}_i>0 \quad\text{for every }1\leq i\leq m.$$
  - Explanation: Simultaneous strict savings for every matrix type would pack into a finite win, contradicting the standing assumption.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-price-word-bounds)): Every nontrivial elementary coordinate shear has price one, including with any number of untouched coordinates. Every invertible $2\times2$ matrix has price at most two. If a word on $w$ coordinates implements $G$, using nonmonomial calls $A_1,\ldots,A_l$ and monomials, then $$p(G)\leq\sum_{j=1}^l p(A_j).$$ If the word instead implements $G\oplus I_s$, the same bound holds for $p(G)$, with no restriction on the initial values of the $s$ coordinates restored by the word.
  - Explanation: Conditional price laws bound elementary shears, pair maps and embedded words, including restored arbitrary scratch.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-corner)): If an invertible matrix $K$ has an invertible square submatrix $M$, then $p(M)\le p(K)$.
  - Explanation: Conditional price cannot increase when restricting to an invertible square submatrix.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-shear-dag)): Suppose a permitted linear DAG with $l$ scalar gates computes $Gx$, with $a$ designated output coordinates. Then $$s(G)\le 8l+2a. \label{corner:eq:shear-dag}$$ If only $a_0$ rows of $G$ are nonzero, $a$ may be replaced by $a_0$. The constant is independent of the dimensions and all circuit scalars.
  - Explanation: Dirty replay gives a rectangular shear price bound independent of circuit coefficients and dimensions.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-convolution)): For every scalar formal series $f$ with $f(0)\ne0$ and every $t\ge1$, $$p(\mathcal T_t(f))=O(t\log(2t)),$$ with an absolute implied constant, independent of the coefficients of $f$ and of their possible dependence on $t$.
  - Explanation: Exact convolution circuits in both directions bound the conditional price of a truncated scalar series map.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-triangular)): If $M\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(\mathbb C)$, $D\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_r(\mathbb C)$ and $C\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_{n\times r}(\mathbb C)$, then $$p\left(\begin{pmatrix}M&C\\0&D\end{pmatrix}\right) \ge p(M)+p(D). \label{corner:eq:triangular}$$ The same conclusion holds for the lower triangular orientation, and the price of any invertible block triangular matrix is at least the sum of the prices of its invertible diagonal blocks.
  - Explanation: Compression of whole contamination blocks proves conditional triangular monotonicity.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-corner-lem-cascade)): For each fixed cell $K$ as in <a href="#corner:eq:cell" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="corner:eq:cell">[corner:eq:cell]</a>, there is a constant $C_K$ such that, for every $k,t\ge1$ and $N=n^k$, $$p\bigl(\mathcal T_t(H^{\otimes k})\bigr) \le tk n^{k-1}p(K) +C_K\bigl(Nt\log(2t)+(k+1)^3N\bigr). \label{corner:eq:cascade-bound}$$ The constant is independent of $t$, $k$, and any scalars used for coefficient interpolation.
  - Explanation: A multi-axis time cascade has a uniform state-correction price bound for each fixed cell.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-dag-price)): If $G\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_m(\mathbb C)$ has a permitted linear DAG with $l$ scalar gates, then $$p(G)\le s(G)\le 8l+2m.$$ The bound holds with the same constants for all dimensions and all matrix families, without requiring a DAG for $G^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Corner contraction and rectangular replay give a one-way conditional price upper bound from a scalar circuit.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-comparison)): Fix $G(z)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_n(\mathbb C[z])$ with $G(0)$ invertible. There is a constant $C_G$ with the following property. Let $k\geq1$, put $N=n^k$ and $d=k\deg G$, and let $t>d$ be an integer. Suppose $\ell(z)$ is monic of degree $t$, $\ell(0)\ne0$, and multiplication by $G(z)^{\otimes k}$ on $(\mathbb C[z]/(\ell))^N$ is invertible. Denote its matrix in the coefficient basis by $W$, and put $$A_G=\mathcal T_t(G(z)^{\otimes k}).$$ Then $$\bigl\|p(W)-p(A_G)\bigr\| \leq C_G N\bigl(t\log(2t)+(k+1)^6\bigr). \label{feedback:eq:boundary-error}$$ The constant is independent of $k,t,\ell$ and of the magnitudes of the coefficients of $\ell$. In particular, for $$t=t(k)=2^{\lceil 8\log_2(k+1)\rceil}, \qquad (k+1)^8\leq t<2(k+1)^8, \label{feedback:eq:common-length}$$ the difference is $o(tkN)$, uniformly over every admissible modulus of that degree.
  - Explanation: Coefficient-boundary changes have a uniformly controlled conditional price discrepancy for admissible scalar moduli.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-feedback)): Let $$K=\begin{pmatrix}M&C\\B&D\end{pmatrix}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{n+r}(\mathbb C), \qquad M\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(\mathbb C), \qquad H(z)=M+zC(I_r-zD)^{-1}B.$$ If $s_0\in\mathbb C$ satisfies $$\det(I_r-s_0D)\ne0,\qquad \det H(s_0)\ne0,$$ then $$p(H(s_0))\leq p(K). \label{feedback:eq:feedback-law}$$ No invertibility of $D$ is required.
  - Explanation: Closing a state connection cannot increase conditional price when the stated denominator and output are invertible.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-specialization)): Let $X(z)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_n(\mathbb C(z))$ be regular at $z_0\in\mathbb C$, and assume $X(z_0)$ is invertible. Suppose there are a finite set $E\subset\mathbb C$ and a real constant $C_0$ such that, for every $s\notin E$, the matrix $X(s)$ is defined and invertible and $$p(X(s))\leq C_0.$$ Then $p(X(z_0))\leq C_0$.
  - Explanation: A generic uniform conditional price bound survives regular invertible algebraic specialization without assuming price continuity.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-pivot-bound)): Let $$K=\begin{pmatrix}A&C\\ B&D\end{pmatrix}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{n+r}(\mathbb C), \qquad A\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(\mathbb C),\quad D\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_r(\mathbb C).$$ Write the action of $K$ as $(x,z_{\rm in})\mapsto(x',z_{\rm out})$. The map $(x,z_{\rm out})\mapsto(x',z_{\rm in})$ is the invertible matrix $$\label{pivot:eq:pivot-matrix} \widetilde K= \begin{pmatrix} A-CD^{-1}B&CD^{-1}\\ -D^{-1}B&D^{-1} \end{pmatrix}, \qquad p(\widetilde K)\le p(K)+4r.$$
  - Explanation: An invertible block pivot has controlled additive conditional price overhead.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pivot-lem-one-port)): Suppose a call to $Y_1\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_a(\mathbb C)$ is followed by a call to $Y_2\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_b(\mathbb C)$, and their ordered coordinate sets intersect in exactly one coordinate. If $P$ is their product on the union of these sets, then $$p(P)=p(Y_1)+p(Y_2).$$ The same equality holds with any additional identity coordinates.
  - Explanation: Successive matrix calls sharing exactly one coordinate have additive conditional price.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-pivot)): Under the hypotheses of <a href="#lem:pivot-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pivot-bound">21</a>, if $r=1$, then $p(\widetilde K)=p(K)$.
  - Explanation: A scalar pivot preserves conditional price exactly when the required blocks are invertible.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pivot-cor-basis-exchange)): Let $U=(u_1,\ldots,u_n,u_*)$ and $V=(v_1,\ldots,v_n,v_*)$ be ordered bases of the same space of linear forms, viewing each list as a column vector of its forms, and let $$V=KU,\qquad K=\begin{pmatrix}A&C\\B&d\end{pmatrix}.$$ The exchanged lists $$U'=(u_1,\ldots,u_n,v_*),\qquad V'=(v_1,\ldots,v_n,u_*)$$ are both bases if and only if $d\ne0$ and $\det A\ne0$. Whenever this holds, the transitions $U\mapsto V$ and $U'\mapsto V'$ have equal price. The statement applies to any chosen member of each basis after reordering them to the last positions.
  - Explanation: Exchanging basis members preserves conditional price precisely when both exchanged lists remain bases.
- **C25** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-price)): Every invertible $2\times2$ matrix all of whose entries are nonzero has price at most $3/2$. In particular, $$\label{pivot:eq:pair-bound} p\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&u\end{pmatrix}\right) \le\frac32\qquad (u\in\mathbb C\setminus\{0,1\}).$$
  - Explanation: Dense invertible pair maps satisfy the stronger conditional price bound through tensor rescaling and legal basis exchanges.
- **C26** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-reflect-triangular-price)): For every integer $m\geq1$, $$p(T_m)=m2^{m-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Scalar exchanges establish the exact conditional price of the signed triangular bit operators.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-reflect-sparse-reflection)): Let $A$ be a real symmetric $w\times w$ matrix with $h$ supported unordered off-diagonal pairs. Let $R$ act as $+I$ on $\ker A$ and as $-I$ on $(\ker A)^\perp$. Then $$p(R)\leq \frac32h+2w.$$
  - Explanation: Sparse polynomial perturbations, feedback and algebraic specialization bound the conditional price of a real kernel reflection.
- **C28** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-win)): There exist an invertible nonmonomial matrix $A$ of a finite width $q$, an integer $b\geq2$, and a word on exactly $q^b$ coordinates implementing $A^{\otimes b}$ with monomials and fewer than $bq^{b-1}$ calls to $A$.
  - Explanation: Incompatible reflection price bounds disprove the no-win assumption and establish existence of a strict finite saving.

          "exact_quote": "If a finite win exists, there are integers $n_m\\to\\infty$ and exact linear circuits for $F_{n_m}$ whose scalar gate counts satisfy $$L(n_m)=o(n_m\\log_2n_m).$$ In particular the conclusion is in the model charging additions, subtractions, and multiplication by every prechosen complex scalar.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-prices",
        "exact_quote": "There is a function $$p:\\bigcup_{n\\geq1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_n(\\mathbb C)\\longrightarrow[0,\\infty)$$ which vanishes exactly on the monomial matrices and has the following properties. For invertible matrices of the indicated sizes, $$\\begin{aligned}\n p(LAR)&=p(A)\n &&\\text{if $L,R$ are monomial of width $|A|$},\n \\label{price:monomial-law}\\\\\n p(AB)&\\leq p(A)+p(B)\n &&\\text{if $|A|=|B|$},\n \\label{price:product-law}\\\\\n p(A\\otimes B)&=|B|p(A)+|A|p(B),\n \\label{price:tensor-law}\\\\\n p(A\\oplus B)&=p(A)+p(B),\n \\label{price:sum-law}\\\\\n p(A^{-1})&=p(A^{\\mathsf T})=p(A).\n \\label{price:symmetry-law}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $$p(U)=1,\\qquad U=\\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\\\1&1\\end{pmatrix}.\n \\label{price:normalization}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-130.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-price-word-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Every nontrivial elementary coordinate shear has price one, including with any number of untouched coordinates. Every invertible $2\\times2$ matrix has price at most two.         {

          "exact_quote": "For each fixed cell $K$ as in <a href=\"#corner:eq:cell\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"corner:eq:cell\">[corner:eq:cell]</a>, there is a constant $C_K$ such that, for every $k,t\\ge1$ and $N=n^k$, $$p\\bigl(\\mathcal T_t(H^{\\otimes k})\\bigr)\n \\le tk n^{k-1}p(K)\n       +C_K\\bigl(Nt\\log(2t)+(k+1)^3N\\bigr).\n \\label{corner:eq:cascade-bound}$$ The constant is independent of $t$, $k$, and any scalars used for coefficient interpolation.",
          "source_id": "PM-130.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-6",
          "exact_quote": "The price function is introduced only under the assumption that there is no finite win.",
          "source_id": "PM-130.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-dag-price",
        "exact_quote": "If $G\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_m(\\mathbb C)$ has a permitted linear DAG with $l$ scalar gates, then $$p(G)\\le s(G)\\le 8l+2m.$$ The bound holds with the same constants for all dimensions and all matrix families, without requiring a DAG for $G^{-1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-130.2"
      },

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $G(z)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mat}}_n(\\mathbb C[z])$ with $G(0)$ invertible. There is a constant $C_G$ with the following property. Let $k\\geq1$, put $N=n^k$ and $d=k\\deg G$, and let $t>d$ be an integer. Suppose $\\ell(z)$ is monic of degree $t$, $\\ell(0)\\ne0$, and multiplication by $G(z)^{\\otimes k}$ on $(\\mathbb C[z]/(\\ell))^N$ is invertible. Denote its matrix in the coefficient basis by $W$, and put $$A_G=\\mathcal T_t(G(z)^{\\otimes k}).$$ Then $$\\bigl|p(W)-p(A_G)\\bigr|\n \\leq C_G N\\bigl(t\\log(2t)+(k+1)^6\\bigr).\n \\label{feedback:eq:boundary-error}$$ The constant is independent of $k,t,\\ell$ and of the magnitudes of the coefficients of $\\ell$. In particular, for $$t=t(k)=2^{\\lceil 8\\log_2(k+1)\\rceil},\n \\qquad (k+1)^8\\leq t<2(k+1)^8,\n \\label{feedback:eq:common-length}$$ the difference is $o(tkN)$, uniformly over every admissible modulus of that degree.",
        "source_id": "PM-130.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

In particular, for $$t=t(k)=2^{\\lceil 8\\log_2(k+1)\\rceil},\n \\qquad (k+1)^8\\leq t<2(k+1)^8,\n \\label{feedback:eq:common-length}$$ the difference is $o(tkN)$, uniformly over every admissible modulus of that degree.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-feedback",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$K=\\begin{pmatrix}M&C\\\\B&D\\end{pmatrix}\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{n+r}(\\mathbb C),\n \\qquad M\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_n(\\mathbb C),\n \\qquad H(z)=M+zC(I_r-zD)^{-1}B.$$ If $s_0\\in\\mathbb C$ satisfies $$\\det(I_r-s_0D)\\ne0,\\qquad \\det H(s_0)\\ne0,$$ then $$p(H(s_0))\\leq p(K).\n \\label{feedback:eq:feedback-law}$$ No invertibility of $D$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-130.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-specialization",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pivot-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$K=\\begin{pmatrix}A&C\\\\ B&D\\end{pmatrix}\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{n+r}(\\mathbb C),\n \\qquad A\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_n(\\mathbb C),\\quad D\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_r(\\mathbb C).$$ Write the action of $K$ as $(x,z_{\\rm in})\\mapsto(x',z_{\\rm out})$. The map $(x,z_{\\rm out})\\mapsto(x',z_{\\rm in})$ is the invertible matrix $$\\label{pivot:eq:pivot-matrix}\n \\widetilde K=\n \\begin{pmatrix}\n A-CD^{-1}B&CD^{-1}\\\\\n -D^{-1}B&D^{-1}\n \\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad p(\\widetilde K)\\le p(K)+4r.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pivot-lem-one-port",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a call to $Y_1\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_a(\\mathbb C)$ is followed by a call to $Y_2\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_b(\\mathbb C)$, and their ordered coordinate sets intersect in exactly one coordinate.       },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite win** ([source](#stmt-def-finite-win)): A finite win consists of an invertible nonmonomial matrix $A\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_q(\mathbb C)$, an integer $b\geq2$, and a word on exactly $q^b$ coordinates implementing $A^{\otimes b}$ with $g$ calls to $A$, where $$\label{gen:eq:strict-win} g<bq^{b-1}.$$
- **Monomial matrix** ([source](#gen:sec:generation)): A *monomial matrix* is a permutation matrix times an invertible diagonal matrix.
- **Matrix call** ([source](#gen:sec:generation)): A *call to $A$ on $w$ coordinates*, where $w\geq \|A\|$, applies $A$ to an ordered tuple of $\|A\|$ distinct coordinates and fixes the others.
- **Matrix word** ([source](#gen:sec:generation)): A *matrix word* is a finite composition of such calls and full-width monomial matrices.
- **Call count** ([source](#gen:sec:generation)): In a word using one specified matrix $A$, its *call count* counts only occurrences of $A$; monomials have zero call count.
- **Pair layer** ([source](#fft:transfer)): A *pair layer* is a direct sum, after a coordinate permutation, of invertible two-coordinate matrices and one-coordinate identities.
- **Monomial layer** ([source](#fft:transfer)): A *monomial layer* is an invertible monomial matrix on the whole coordinate set.
- **Minimum Fourier gate count** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $L(n)$ be the minimum number of gates in such a circuit.
- **Rectangular shear price** ([source](#pm-h-24)): For $G\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_{a\times b}(\mathbb C)$ put $$s(G)=p\left(\begin{pmatrix}I_a&G\\0&I_b\end{pmatrix}\right).$$
- **Truncated series multiplication** ([source](#pm-h-24)): For a scalar or matrix formal power series $F(z)$, let $\mathcal T_t(F)$ be multiplication by $F(z)$ modulo $z^t$, in the coefficient basis ordered with time first.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Normalized Fourier gate-count limit infimum | = | $0$ | Unbounded sequence of lengths; no all-length upper bound. |
| Q2 | Finite-win call threshold | < | $bq^{b-1}$ | A specified finite win with an invertible nonmonomial matrix and exact word. |
| Q3 | Amplified tensor scalar gate count | ≤ | $Cq^k(k+1)^\alpha$ | A specified finite win; every nonnegative integer tensor order. |
| Q4 | Dirty replay shear count | ≤ | $8\ell+2a$ | A finite linear DAG with bounded fan-out, disjoint source and target, and arbitrary scratch. |
| Q5 | Triangular Toeplitz layer count | ≤ | $O(1+\log^4 r)$ | Invertible lower triangular Toeplitz matrices; absolute entry-independent implied constant. |
| Q6 | Exact-width Fourier layer count | ≤ | $O(1+\log^4r)$ | Every positive integer width; absolute implied constant. |
| Q7 | Rectangular shear price | ≤ | $8l+2a$ | A permitted scalar DAG and its designated output coordinates. |
| Q8 | Universal one-way DAG price | ≤ | $8l+2m$ | Invertible circuit output; uniform in matrix family and dimensions. |
| Q9 | Dense pair price | ≤ | $3/2$ | Invertible pair matrices with every entry nonzero. |
| Q10 | Signed triangular operator price | = | $m2^{m-1}$ | Every positive integer bit order. |
| Q11 | Real kernel reflection price | ≤ | $\frac32h+2w$ | Real symmetric matrix with the stated number of supported unordered off-diagonal pairs. |
| Q12 | Combined hypothetical reflection price ceiling | ≤ | $\left(\frac{3d}{4}+\frac{15}{2}\right)w$ | No finite win, fixed positive integer parameter and positive width. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-generation): Full-rank diagonal differential, an actual Zariski-open image and products of open-set values. -> A fixed positive call pattern realizes every invertible target of the fixed width by adjusting its monomial factors.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-amplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-amplification): Tensor sector decomposition and induction with a finite binomial weighted sum. -> A specified strict finite saving amplifies to a sublinear dependence on tensor order after charging scalar gates.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-fft-dirty-layers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fft-dirty-layers): Forward and inverse sweeps followed by source-free replay cancel arbitrary scratch contamination. -> Forward replay, reverse replay and cancellation add the linear output while restoring arbitrary borrowed values.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-fft-toeplitz-layers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fft-toeplitz-layers): Low displacement rank, convolution chunks and balanced recursion on existing coordinates. -> Low displacement rank and restored internal workspace yield shallow Toeplitz factorizations on the original coordinates.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-layers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-layers): Newton evaluation and uniqueness of triangular factorization. -> Newton interpolation and triangular Toeplitz factors give an exact Fourier factorization without widening the coordinate set.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-win-to-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-win-to-fourier): Coprime tensor permutations and synchronized positive call patterns. -> Synchronization across distinct prime factors transfers any supplied finite win to a subsequence of exact Fourier circuits.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-price-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-price-packing): Disjoint tensor sectors, randomized starts and chronological identity vacancy filling. -> Simultaneous strict savings for every matrix type would pack into a finite win, contradicting the standing assumption.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-prices) / [proof](#pm-h-21): Separation, normalized compact intervals, compactness and inverse-transpose averaging. -> Assuming no finite win, separation and compactness construct an auxiliary price satisfying the displayed algebraic laws.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-shear-dag) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shear-dag): Implement a rectangular shear by restored dirty-coordinate replay. -> Dirty replay gives a rectangular shear price bound independent of circuit coefficients and dimensions.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-convolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-convolution): Use scalar convolution DAGs in both directions. -> Exact convolution circuits in both directions bound the conditional price of a truncated scalar series map.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-lem-triangular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-triangular): Compress whole contamination row blocks and cancel sparse contributions. -> Compression of whole contamination blocks proves conditional triangular monotonicity.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-corner-lem-cascade) / [proof](#proof-stmt-corner-lem-cascade): Clear the rational state response and interpolate polynomial coefficient maps. -> A multi-axis time cascade has a uniform state-correction price bound for each fixed cell.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-thm-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-corner-lem-cascade-2): Normalize long tensor cascades and let their finite price error vanish. -> Conditional price cannot increase when restricting to an invertible square submatrix.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-cor-dag-price) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-dag-price): Contract an invertible circuit output from its rectangular shear. -> Corner contraction and rectangular replay give a one-way conditional price upper bound from a scalar circuit.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-comparison): Prefix conjugation restricts the correction to active times with ordered spatial generators. -> Coefficient-boundary changes have a uniformly controlled conditional price discrepancy for admissible scalar moduli.
- **R19** [claim](#stmt-thm-feedback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-feedback): Compare coefficient boundaries with Taylor translation and triangular monotonicity. -> Closing a state connection cannot increase conditional price when the stated denominator and output are invertible.
- **R20** [claim](#stmt-thm-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-specialization): Evaluate at admissible dilated roots and compare exact coefficient boundary prices. -> A generic uniform conditional price bound survives regular invertible algebraic specialization without assuming price continuity.
- **R21** [claim](#stmt-lem-pivot-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pivot-bound): Realize the block solve as a legal feedback output. -> An invertible block pivot has controlled additive conditional price overhead.
- **R22** [claim](#stmt-pivot-lem-one-port) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pivot-lem-one-port): Group consecutive calls on a tensor-grid cross. -> Successive matrix calls sharing exactly one coordinate have additive conditional price.
- **R23** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-pivot) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-pivot): Compare repeated forward and pivoted scalar-state chains. -> A scalar pivot preserves conditional price exactly when the required blocks are invertible.
- **R24** [claim](#stmt-pivot-cor-basis-exchange) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pivot-cor-basis-exchange): Check both exchanged lists remain bases before a scalar pivot. -> Exchanging basis members preserves conditional price precisely when both exchanged lists remain bases.
- **R25** [claim](#stmt-lem-pair-price) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-price): Legal basis exchanges, rank-one perturbation and monomial tensor rescaling. -> Dense invertible pair maps satisfy the stronger conditional price bound through tensor rescaling and legal basis exchanges.
- **R26** [claim](#stmt-reflect-triangular-price) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reflect-triangular-price): Exchange bottom coordinates with principal-minor checks and signed recursion. -> Scalar exchanges establish the exact conditional price of the signed triangular bit operators.
- **R27** [claim](#stmt-reflect-sparse-reflection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reflect-sparse-reflection): Sparse polynomial perturbation followed by two algebraic specializations. -> Sparse polynomial perturbations, feedback and algebraic specialization bound the conditional price of a real kernel reflection.
- **R28** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite-win) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-finite-win): Recover involution price from its graph reflection and derive incompatible bounds. -> Incompatible reflection price bounds disprove the no-win assumption and establish existence of a strict finite saving.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-finite-win): Combine finite-win existence with subsequential Fourier transfer. -> The normalized scalar gate count has vanishing limit infimum along an unbounded sequence in the unrestricted exact nonuniform model.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The replay adds the supplied linear map to the targets and restores the sources and scratch coordinates.
- **I2**: Each scalar multiplication on variable data is charged in the Fourier circuit model.
- **I3**: The price laws are conditional auxiliary comparisons and do not assert a lower bound for circuit size.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Replace an arbitrary-length Fourier routine with the existence result. -> A uniform all-length construction is not supplied by the quantified conclusion.
- **A2**: Infer numerical stability or bit complexity from the gate bound. -> Arithmetic gate counts do not bound coefficient descriptions or conditioning.
- **A3**: Treat monomial factors as free scalar operations in an implemented circuit. -> Word call counts omit monomials while scalar gate counts charge their multiplications.
- **A4**: Use an auxiliary price as an unconditional circuit lower bound. -> The construction assumes the absence of a finite win, which the paper contradicts.
- **A5**: Read an executable replay check as a certificate for the Fourier existence theorem. -> A supplied DAG replay does not exhibit a winning tensor word.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Dirty-coordinate replay is a finite constructive result for a supplied DAG. This executable instance concerns that replay alone; the main Fourier result remains nonuniform existence along an unbounded sequence.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON cases containing rational source, target, scratch, gates and output indices.
# PRECONDITIONS: Bounded topologically ordered scalar linear DAG; disjoint coordinate groups.
# OUTPUTS: Restored source and scratch, accumulated target, and an explicit shear word.
# TIME: Linear in gates and output reads per case, excluding exact rational bit costs.
# SPACE: Linear in coordinates and emitted shears per case.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic using fmpq; no rounding.
# FAILURE: Reject malformed or oversized data; no partial certificate.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpq

def replay(case):
    e, a, ell = len(case['x']), len(case['y']), len(case['gates'])
    assert 1 <= e <= 4 and 0 <= a <= 4 and ell <= 8
    assert len(case['z']) == ell and len(case['outputs']) == a
    def rational(value):
        assert isinstance(value, str) and len(value) <= 32
        return fmpq(value)
    values = [rational(v) for v in case['x'] + case['y'] + case['z']]
    def physical(index):
        assert isinstance(index, int) and -1 <= index < e + ell
        return index if index < e else index + a
    forward = []
    for v, gate in enumerate(case['gates']):
        op = gate['op']
        assert op in ('add', 'sub', 'mul')
        args = gate['args']
        assert len(args) == (1 if op == 'mul' else 2)
        weights = [rational(gate['scalar'])] if op == 'mul' else [fmpq(1), fmpq(-1 if op == 'sub' else 1)]
        for arg, weight in zip(args, weights):
            assert isinstance(arg, int) and -1 <= arg < e + v
            if arg != -1:
                forward.append((e + a + v, physical(arg), weight))
    reads = [(e + j, physical(out), fmpq(1)) for j, out in enumerate(case['outputs']) if out != -1]
    dirty = [step for step in forward if step[1] >= e + a]
    cleanup_reads = [step for step in reads if step[1] >= e + a]
    def inverse(steps):
        return [(dst, src, -coef) for dst, src, coef in reversed(steps)]
    word = forward + reads + inverse(forward) + dirty + inverse(cleanup_reads) + inverse(dirty)
    for dst, src, coef in word:
        assert dst != src
        values[dst] += coef * values[src]
    assert len(word) <= 8 * ell + 2 * a
    return {'values': [str(v) for v in values], 'word': [[d, s, str(c)] for d, s, c in word]}

batch = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert isinstance(batch['cases'], list) and 1 <= len(batch['cases']) <= 1000
print(json.dumps([replay(case) for case in batch['cases']], separators=(',', ':')))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The dirty-coordinate replay identity can guide exact linear computation with borrowed workspace when a concrete linear DAG is supplied. This is an adaptation of an auxiliary construction; the Fourier existence theorem supplies no general circuit generator. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Application to games or simulation is ABSENT. Exact transform operation counts along a subsequence do not supply an approximate numerical simulation method. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A software tool interface or executable Fourier circuit generator is ABSENT. The main theorem gives nonuniform existence and leaves coefficient preparation outside its model. |
| algorithms | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The paper directly informs exact Fourier algorithm research by connecting a strict finite tensor-call saving to subsequential scalar-gate savings. The existence proof does not identify a winning matrix and word for deployment. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A data-structure operation contract is ABSENT. Tensor sectors and coordinate decompositions are parts of matrix identities; no lookup, update, or storage complexity is established. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A hardware execution contract is ABSENT. The model permits unrestricted depth and storage and counts exact scalar operations rather than physical execution costs. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A cryptographic construction or security reduction is ABSENT. The Fourier result concerns unrestricted complex scalar arithmetic without a bit-complexity guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning objective, training method, or inference guarantee is ABSENT. Exact Fourier circuit existence does not supply an approximate numerical learning procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The packing proof offers a mathematical scheduling idea: preserve the order inside each program while reserving capacity for every call type and filling vacancies with identity words. An operations-research solver or finite-instance optimization guarantee is ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A distributed protocol or communication model is ABSENT. Parallel tensor-axis reasoning concerns exact matrix operations and does not specify networked execution. |
| other (Algebraic circuit complexity) | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The result directly supports algebraic circuit-complexity research. Finite-win existence closes the Fourier lower-bound contradiction; matrix prices are auxiliary objects introduced only under the opposite assumption. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (nonconstructive): The Fourier conclusion is nonuniform existence along an unbounded sequence of lengths; it supplies neither a uniform generator nor an all-length upper bound. Blocked use: ['Claiming a circuit generator for arbitrary transform lengths.', 'Replacing a production Fourier routine solely on this existence theorem.']
- **L2** (formal_scope): Coefficient preparation is outside the operation-count model, and coefficient magnitude, algebraic degree, and description length are unrestricted. Blocked use: ['Claiming bounded-coefficient complexity or a bound on coefficient preparation.']
- **L3** (approximation): The conclusion gives no numerical stability or bit-complexity guarantee. Blocked use: ['Claiming a floating-point speedup with controlled error.', 'Transferring the arithmetic bound to bit complexity.']
- **L4** (formal_scope): Depth, storage, cancellation, and conditioning are unrestricted; the result does not bound these execution resources. Blocked use: ['Claiming bounded storage, circuit depth, or conditioning.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): Circuit structure and coefficients may depend on the transform length but must be independent of the input. Blocked use: ['Choosing circuit coefficients after observing the input.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): Every output must equal the Fourier transform exactly for every complex input. Blocked use: ['Substituting an approximate transform or checking only selected inputs.']
- **L7** (counterexample_scope): Existence of a finite win does not assert a finite win for every invertible nonmonomial matrix. Blocked use: ['Assuming a supplied nonmonomial matrix admits a winning tensor word.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): Auxiliary prices are introduced under the assumption that no finite win exists and are not circuit-size lower bounds. Blocked use: ['Treating auxiliary prices as unconditional costs of executable circuits.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Dirty-coordinate replay requires a supplied linear DAG and disjoint source, target, and scratch coordinates; scratch values may be arbitrary. Blocked use: ['Applying replay with overlapping source, target, or scratch coordinates.', 'Claiming that replay constructs the existential Fourier circuits.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The feedback price comparison requires both an invertible feedback denominator and an invertible output matrix. Blocked use: ['Applying feedback at a singular denominator or singular output.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): Algebraic specialization requires regularity and invertibility at the target value; it does not assume continuity of the auxiliary price. Blocked use: ['Specializing a rational matrix at a nonremovable pole or singular target.', 'Inferring price continuity from specialization.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): Transpose denotes ordinary transpose; the reflection argument uses real symmetry and real orthogonality explicitly. Blocked use: ['Replacing ordinary transpose by conjugate transpose throughout the identities.', 'Extending the real-symmetric reflection argument to arbitrary complex matrices without proof.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): Both diagonal blocks in the pivot lemma must be invertible: one permits solving for the state and the other makes the transition invertible. Blocked use: ['Applying the pivot price bound when a required diagonal block is singular.']

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 130 (`lean/docs/130.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Finite tensor savings and exact Fourier circuits](../../preprints/Finite-tensor-savings-and-exact-Fourier-circuits-September-25-2026/main.pdf)
> - [An explicit power saving for the exact discrete Fourier transform](../../preprints/An-explicit-power-saving-for-the-exact-discrete-Fourier-transform-September-25-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives exact discrete Fourier transforms with arbitrarily small normalized circuit cost along an unbounded sequence of lengths. For every $c>0$ and every cutoff $N_0\ge2$, some $n\ge N_0$ has a circuit computing the unnormalized DFT with fewer than $cn\log_2 n$ gates. Addition, subtraction, and multiplication by a predetermined complex scalar each cost one gate; diagonal scalings are charged. The result is subsequential, with no all-length, bounded-coefficient, conditioning, or bit-complexity claim.
>
> A separate uniform formalization gives fixed deterministic programs for every positive length $n$, computing the canonical discrete Fourier transform and the full convolution of two length-$n$ complex vectors exactly. Their work is $O(n(\log n)^{1-10^{-13}})=o(n\log n)$, including root-order selection and scalar preparation. The chosen root of unity is supplied; the model permits exact complex arithmetic and unrestricted coefficients, while integer values and indices remain polynomially bounded in $n$. The synthesis appendix's extraction, search, and printer uniformization is outside these selected statements.

Comparator source `ExactFourier.lean` (2005 bytes, sha256 `93b33a2b9f6543dd0b893c3a5a504df19a1a39476072c6d7ebe18133a820ef6d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
Scalar circuits for exact discrete Fourier transforms.
No bounds on predetermined complex coefficients, reuse, storage, or conditioning.
-/

namespace ExactFourier

/-- One charged scalar gate, referencing only available values. -/
inductive Gate (w : ℕ) where
  | add (i j : Fin w)
  | sub (i j : Fin w)
  | scale (c : ℂ) (i : Fin w)

def Gate.eval {w : ℕ} (v : Fin w → ℂ) : Gate w → ℂ
  | .add i j => v i + v j
  | .sub i j => v i - v j
  | .scale c i => c * v i

/-- A topologically ordered scalar DAG; available values are never consumed. -/
inductive Program (n : ℕ) : ℕ → Type where
  | nil : Program n 0
  | step {k : ℕ} (p : Program n k) (g : Gate (n + 1 + k)) : Program n (k + 1)

def Program.eval {n : ℕ} : {k : ℕ} → Program n k → (Fin n → ℂ) →
    (Fin (n + 1 + k) → ℂ)
  | 0, .nil, x => Fin.snoc x 0
  | _ + 1, .step p g, x =>
      let v := p.eval x
      Fin.snoc v (g.eval v)

/-- Outputs name available values, so permutations and arbitrary fanout are uncharged. -/
structure Circuit (n : ℕ) where
  size : ℕ
  program : Program n size
  outputs : Fin n → Fin (n + 1 + size)

def Circuit.eval {n : ℕ} (C : Circuit n) (x : Fin n → ℂ) : Fin n → ℂ :=
  fun i => C.program.eval x (C.outputs i)

noncomputable def zeta (n : ℕ) : ℂ := Complex.exp (2 * Real.pi * Complex.I / (n : ℂ))
noncomputable def fourierMatrix (n : ℕ) : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ :=
  fun j k => zeta n ^ (j.val * k.val)

def Circuit.Computes {n : ℕ} (C : Circuit n) (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℂ) : Prop :=
  ∀ x, C.eval x = A.mulVec x

/-- The precise quantified formulation of source theorem `thm:main`. -/
def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ c : ℝ, 0 < c → ∀ N₀ : ℕ, 2 ≤ N₀ → ∃ n : ℕ, N₀ ≤ n ∧
    ∃ C : Circuit n, C.Computes (fourierMatrix n) ∧
      (C.size : ℝ) < c * (n : ℝ) * Real.logb 2 (n : ℝ)

theorem main_theorem : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end ExactFourier

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct exact nonuniform Fourier circuits of size $`o(n\log n)`$ along an unbounded sequence of lengths, counting every addition, subtraction, and scalar multiplication. This refutes the $`\Omega(n\log n)`$ lower bound in the unrestricted complex linear-circuit model. The construction uses a finite tensor saving: a tensor power of some invertible nonmonomial complex matrix can be computed with fewer matrix calls than the standard tensor-axis algorithm on the same coordinates, when invertible monomial maps are allowed freely between calls.
